E_{0,l}(t)\lesssim\delta^{4-2\varepsilon_{0}},\quad l\leq 4.

(7.387)

2.

The case
p+l\leq 4
and
p\geq 1

For
p+l\leq 4

(p\geq 1)
, as in the above case, one can make use of Proposition
7.1
and Corollary
7.1
to estimate the
related integrals in (
7.382
) for
\max\{p_{1}+l_{1},\cdots,p_{k}+l_{k}\}\leq p_{0}+l_{0}
and
q_{1}+j_{1}\leq q_{0}+j_{0}
(
p_{1}+l_{1},\cdots,p_{k}+l_{k}\leq 2
,
p_{0}+l_{0}\leq 3
,
q_{1}+j_{1}\leq 2
and
q_{0}+j_{0}\leq 3
) and the rest terms (
p_{0}+l_{0}\leq 1
or
q_{0}+j_{0}\leq 1
). In the whole process,
one can use repeatedly the bootstrap assumptions
7.371
and the restricted condition
\varepsilon_{0}\in(0,\varepsilon_{k}^{*})
for
k\geq 2
.

Consequently, by Grownwall’s inequality, one can arrive at

\begin{split}&E_{0,l}(t)\lesssim\delta^{4-2\varepsilon_{0}},\qquad E_{1,l}(t)\lesssim\delta^{17/6-2\varepsilon_{0}},\qquad E_{2,l}(t)\lesssim\delta^{1-2\varepsilon_{0}},\\
&E_{3,l}(t)\lesssim\delta^{-1-2\varepsilon_{0}},\qquad E_{4,l}(t)\lesssim\delta^{-3-2\varepsilon_{0}}.\end{split}

(7.388)

Since the constants in (
7.388
) are all independent of
M_{0}
, the bootstrap
assumptions (
7.371
) are proved as long as (
7.371
) holds for the time
t=t_{0}

by the continuous induction argument.

∎

We are now ready to prove Theorem
1.1
.

Proof.

By Theorem
2.1
, the local existence of the smooth solution
\phi
to (
1.1
) with (
1.2
),
(
1.14
) and
(
1.15
)-(
1.16
) has been obtained.
On the other hand, the global estimates of
\phi
in
A_{2\delta}

and
B_{2\delta}
have been established in Section
6
and Section
7
respectively.
Then it follows from the local well-posedness of the smooth solution to (
1.1
) with (
1.2
) (see
[
13
]
or
[
14
]
)
and the continuous induction argument
that
\phi\in C^{\infty}([1,+\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{2})
exists globally.
In addition,
|\partial\phi|\lesssim\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}t^{-1/2}
follows from (
B.12
) in Appendix B, (
6.312
) and
the first inequality in (
7.372
). Meanwhile,
|\phi|\lesssim\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}t^{1/2}
holds true by integration
on
\partial\phi
from the conic surface
C_{0}
.
Thus Theorem
1.1
is proved.
∎

Appendix A
Computations on the deformation tensor and commutators relations

Let
{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(V)}\not{\pi}_{UX}:={\vphantom{\pi}}^{(V)}\pi_{UX}
for
U\in\{\mathring{L},\underline{\mathring{L}},X\}
.
As in
[
23
, Proposition 7.7]
for 3D case or in
[
10
, (2.43)-(2.45)]
for 4D case,
it follows from direct computations that

(1)

for
V=T
,

\begin{split}&{\vphantom{\pi}}{}^{(T)}\pi_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}=0,\quad{\vphantom{\pi}}^{(T)}\pi_{T\tilde{T}}=2T\mu,\quad{\vphantom{\pi}}^{(T)}\pi_{\mathring{L}T}=-T\mu,\quad{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}^{(T)}\not{\pi}_{TX}=0,\\
&{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(T)}\not{\pi}_{\mathring{L}X}=-2\mu\zeta_{X}-\not{d}_{X}\mu,\quad{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}^{(T)}\not{\pi}_{XX}=2\mu\sigma_{XX};\end{split}

(A.2)

(2)

for
V=\mathring{L}
,

\begin{split}&{\vphantom{\pi}}{}^{(\mathring{L})}\pi_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}=0,\quad{\vphantom{\pi}}^{(\mathring{L})}\pi_{T\tilde{T}}=2\mathring{L}\mu,\quad{\vphantom{\pi}}^{(\mathring{L})}\pi_{\mathring{L}T}=-\mathring{L}\mu,\quad{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}^{(\mathring{L})}\not{\pi}_{\mathring{L}X}=0,\\
&{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(\mathring{L})}\not{\pi}_{TX}=2\mu\zeta_{X}+\not{d}_{X}\mu,\quad{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}^{(\mathring{L})}\not{\pi}_{XX}=2\chi_{XX};\end{split}

(A.3)

(3)

for
V=R
,

\begin{split}&{\vphantom{\pi}}{}^{(R)}\pi_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}=0,\quad{\vphantom{\pi}}^{(R)}\pi_{T\tilde{T}}=2R\mu,\quad{\vphantom{\pi}}^{(R)}\pi_{\mathring{L}T}=-R\mu,\\
&{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(R)}\not{\pi}_{\mathring{L}X}=-{R}^{X}\check{\chi}_{XX}-\upsilon\{G_{\mathring{L}\tilde{T}}^{\gamma}\not{d}_{X}\varphi_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2}G_{\tilde{T}\tilde{T}}^{\gamma}\not{d}_{X}\varphi_{\gamma}-G_{X\tilde{T}}^{\gamma}{\mathring{L}}\varphi_{\gamma}\}\\
&\qquad\qquad\quad+\frac{1}{2}{R}^{X}G_{XX}^{\gamma}\mathring{L}\varphi_{\gamma}+\epsilon_{i}^{j}g_{ja}\check{L}^{i}\not{d}_{X}x^{a},\\
&{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(R)}\not{\pi}_{TX}=\mu{R}^{X}\check{\chi}_{XX}+\upsilon\not{d}_{X}\mu+\mu G_{X\mathring{L}}^{\gamma}R\varphi_{\gamma}-\frac{1}{2}\mu R^{X}G_{XX}^{\gamma}{\mathring{L}}\varphi_{\gamma}\\
&\qquad\qquad\quad+\mu G_{X\tilde{T}}^{\gamma}R\varphi_{\gamma}+\upsilon\{G_{X\tilde{T}}^{\gamma}T\varphi_{\gamma}-\frac{1}{2}\mu G_{\tilde{T}\tilde{T}}^{\gamma}\not{d}_{X}\varphi_{\gamma}\}+\mu\epsilon_{i}^{j}g_{ja}\check{T}^{i}\not{d}_{X}x^{a},\\
&{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(R)}\not{\pi}_{XX}=2\upsilon\chi_{XX}+\upsilon\{2G_{X\mathring{L}}^{\gamma}\not{d}_{X}\varphi_{\gamma}+2G_{X\tilde{T}}^{\gamma}\not{d}_{X}\varphi_{\gamma}-G_{XX}^{\gamma}\mathring{L}\varphi_{\gamma}\}\\
&\qquad\qquad\quad+G_{XX}^{\gamma}R\varphi_{\gamma}+2\epsilon_{i}^{j}\check{g}_{ja}(\not{d}_{X}x^{a})\not{d}_{X}x^{i},\end{split}

(A.4)

where
\check{g}_{ia}:=g_{ia}-m_{ia}
, and the definitions of
\check{T}^{i}
,
\check{L}^{i}
are given in (
2.30
).

In addition, the following two results hold for commutators,
which are given in Lemma 4.10, 8.9 and 8.11 of
[
23
]
.

Lemma A.1
.

It holds that

\begin{split}[\mathring{L},R]&={{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(R)}\not{\pi}_{\mathring{L}}}^{X}X,\quad[\mathring{L},T]={{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(T)}\not{\pi}_{\mathring{L}}}^{X}X,\quad[T,R]={{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(R)}\not{\pi}_{T}}^{X}X.\end{split}

(A.5)

Lemma A.2
.

For any vector field
Z\in\{\mathring{L},T,R\}
,

(a) if
f
is a smooth function, then

\displaystyle\big([\not{\nabla}^{2},\not{\mathcal{L}}_{Z}]f\big)_{XX}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}{\not{\nabla}}_{X}\big(\textrm{tr}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}^{(Z)}\not{\pi}\big)\not{d}_{X}f,

(A.6)

\displaystyle[\not{\triangle},Z]f

\displaystyle={\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}^{(Z)}\not{\pi}^{XX}\not{\nabla}_{X}^{2}f+\frac{1}{2}({\not{\nabla}}_{X}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}^{(Z)}\not{\pi}^{XX})\not{d}_{X}f;

(A.7)

(b) if
\xi
is a one-form on
S_{s,u}
, then

([\not{\nabla}_{X},\not{\mathcal{L}}_{Z}]\xi)_{X}=\frac{1}{2}{\not{\nabla}}_{X}(\textrm{tr}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}^{(Z)}\not{\pi})\xi_{X};

(A.8)

(c) if
\xi
is a
(0,2)
-type tensor on
S_{s,u}
, then

\displaystyle([\not{\nabla}_{X},\not{\mathcal{L}}_{Z}]\xi)_{XX}

\displaystyle={\not{\nabla}}_{X}\big(\textrm{tr}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}^{(Z)}\not{\pi}\big)\xi_{XX},

(A.9)

\displaystyle([\not{\nabla}_{X},\not{\mathcal{L}}_{Z}]\not{\nabla}\xi)_{XXX}

\displaystyle=\frac{3}{2}{\not{\nabla}}_{X}\big(\textrm{tr}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}^{(Z)}\not{\pi}\big)\not{\nabla}_{X}\xi_{XX}.

(A.10)

Appendix B
The proof of Theorem
2.1

In this Appendix, we utilize the energy method and the special structure of the initial data (
1.2
)
together with (
1.14
) and
(
1.15
)-(
1.16
)
to prove Theorem
2.1
.
Although the procedure is similar
to that in
[
11
, Theorem 2.1]
,
some details are required to be given due to the general form of the quasilinear wave
equation (
1.1
) with the higher order null condition structure here.

Proof.

Denote by
Z_{g}
any fixed vector field in
\{S,H_{i},i=1,2\}
.
Then analogously to the proof of (2.5) in
[
11
]
, one can have that for
1\leq t\leq t_{0}
,
\nu\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{3}

and
N_{0}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
with
N_{0}\geq 3
,

|\partial^{\kappa}\Omega^{p}Z_{g}^{\nu}\phi|\leq\delta^{2-\epsilon_{0}-|\kappa|}\quad(|\kappa|+p+|\nu|\leq N_{0},\quad|\nu|\leq 1).

(B.11)

In addition, by
\displaystyle L=\frac{S+\omega^{i}H_{i}}{t+r}
, it can be shown that for
|\kappa|+p+m\leq 2N_{0}-3
and
m\leq 1
,

|L^{m}\partial^{\kappa}\Omega^{p}\phi(t,x)|\lesssim\sum_{|\nu|\leq 1}|Z_{g}^{\nu}\partial^{\kappa}\Omega^{p}\phi(t,x)|\lesssim\delta^{2-|\kappa|-\varepsilon_{0}}.

(B.12)

Figure 3:
Space-time domain
D^{1}=\{(t,r):1\leq t\leq t_{0},2-t\leq r\leq t\}

Now we start to improve the
L^{\infty}
estimate of
\phi(t_{0},x)
on some special space domains. To this end,
one rewrites (
1.1
) as

L\underline{L}\phi=\frac{1}{2r}L\phi-\frac{1}{2r}\underline{L}\phi+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\Omega^{2}\phi+g^{\alpha\beta,\gamma_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{k}}(\partial_{\gamma_{1}}\phi)\cdots(\partial_{\gamma_{k}}\phi)\partial^{2}_{\alpha\beta}\phi+h^{\alpha\beta}(\partial\phi)\partial^{2}_{\alpha\beta}\phi.

(B.13)

Applying the operator
L\bar{\partial}^{\iota}\Omega^{p}
to both sides of (
B.13
), where
\bar{\partial}\in\{\partial_{t},\partial_{r}\}
and
\iota\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{2}
, then one can obtain an expression of
\underline{L}L^{2}\bar{\partial}^{\iota}\Omega^{p}\phi
by using
\underline{L}L^{2}\bar{\partial}^{\iota}\Omega^{p}=L\bar{\partial}^{\iota}\Omega^{p}L\underline{L}

and a direct computation.
This, together with (
B.12
), yields that for
|\iota|+p\leq 2N_{0}-6
,

|\underline{L}(L^{2}\bar{\partial}^{\iota}\Omega^{p}\phi)|\lesssim\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}-|\iota|}+\delta^{(k+1)(1-\varepsilon_{0})-1-|\iota|}.

(B.14)

Due to the vanishing property of
\phi
on
\{(t,x):t\geq 1,t=r\}
,
integrating (
B.14
) along integral curves of
\underline{L}
yields that for
(t,r)\in D^{1}

(see Figure
3
) and for
|\iota|+p\leq 2N_{0}-6
,

|L^{2}\bar{\partial}^{\iota}\Omega^{p}\phi(t,x)|\lesssim\delta^{2-\varepsilon_{0}-|\iota|}+\delta^{(k+1)(1-\varepsilon_{0})-|\iota|}.

(B.15)

Note that the null condition (
1.9
) implies

\begin{split}g^{\alpha\beta,\gamma_{1}\cdots\gamma_{k}}\partial_{\gamma_{1}}\phi\cdots\partial_{\gamma_{k}}\phi\partial_{\alpha\beta}^{2}\phi=&f_{1}(\omega)L\phi(\partial\phi)^{k-1}\partial^{2}\phi+f_{2}(\omega)r^{-1}\Omega\phi(\partial\phi)^{k-1}\partial^{2}\phi\\
&+f_{3}(\omega)(\partial\phi)^{k}L\partial\phi+f_{4}(\omega)r^{-1}(\partial\phi)^{k}\Omega\partial\phi,\end{split}

(B.16)

where
f_{i}

(i=1,2,3,4)
are some smooth functions.
Using the equation for
\underline{L}L^{2}\bar{\partial}^{\iota}\Omega^{p}\phi
again, together with (
B.16
) and
(
B.15
), gives
|\underline{L}(L^{2}\bar{\partial}^{\iota}\Omega^{p}\phi)|\lesssim\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}-|\iota|}
for
0<\varepsilon_{0}<\varepsilon_{k}^{*}
and
|\iota|+p\leq 2N_{0}-7
, which yields that for
(t,x)\in D^{1}
,

|L^{2}\bar{\partial}^{\iota}\Omega^{p}\phi(t,x)|\lesssim\delta^{2-\varepsilon_{0}-|\iota|},\quad|\iota|+p\leq 2N_{0}-7.

(B.17)

Then an induction argument and (
B.13
) show that for
(t,x)\in D^{1}
,

|LL^{m}\bar{\partial}^{\iota}\Omega^{p}\phi(t,x)|\lesssim\delta^{2-|\iota|-\varepsilon_{0}},\quad 3m+|\iota|+p\leq 2N_{0}-4,

(B.18)

which implies (
2.50
) due to
\partial_{i}=\omega^{i}\partial_{r}+\frac{\omega_{\perp}^{i}}{r}\Omega
.

Figure 4:
Spatial domain for
1-3\delta\leq r\leq 1+\delta
on
\Sigma_{1+2\delta}

Similarly, by the expression of
L\underline{L}^{n}\bar{\partial}^{\iota}\Omega^{p}\phi
, integrating it
along integral curves of
L
yields that for
r\in[1-3\delta,1+\delta]
(see Figure
4
),

|\underline{L}^{n}\bar{\partial}^{\iota}\Omega^{p}\phi(t_{0},x)|\lesssim\delta^{2-|\iota|-\varepsilon_{0}},\quad 2n+|\iota|+p\leq 2N_{0}-3,

(B.19)

which implies (
2.51
).
In addition, it follows from (
B.12
) that

|\Omega^{p}\phi|_{D^{2}}\lesssim\delta^{3-\varepsilon_{0}},\ p\leq 2N_{0}-4

(B.20)

after integrating
L\Omega^{k}\phi
along integral curves of
L
in
D^{2}
.
Furthermore, using
\partial_{t}=\frac{1}{2}(L+\underline{L})
,

\partial_{i}=\frac{\omega^{i}}{2}(L-\underline{L})+\frac{\omega^{i}_{\perp}}{r}\Omega
,
(
B.19
), (
B.20
), (
B.13
) and (
B.12
), one concludes that for
|\kappa|+p\leq 2N_{0}-5

and
r\in[1-3\delta,1+\delta]
,

|\partial^{\kappa}\Omega^{p}\phi(t_{0},x)|\lesssim\delta^{3-|\kappa|-\varepsilon_{0}},

(B.21)

and then (
2.52
) follows.

Next we prove the estimate (
2.53
). It is already shown that on the surface
\Sigma_{t_{0}}
, when
r\in[1-2\delta,1+\delta]
and
2n+|\kappa|+p\leq 2N_{0}-3
,
|\underline{L}^{n}\partial^{\kappa}\Omega^{p}\phi|\lesssim\delta^{2-\varepsilon_{0}-|\kappa|}
holds. Then
|L\underline{L}^{n}\partial^{\kappa}\Omega^{p}\phi|\lesssim\delta^{2-\varepsilon_{0}-|\kappa|}
is derived by (
B.13
) and a direct computation.
Meanwhile, when
r\in[1-2\delta,1+\delta]
and
3m+|\kappa|+p\leq 2N_{0}-4
,
one has
|L^{1+m}\partial^{\kappa}\Omega^{p}\phi|\lesssim\delta^{2-\varepsilon_{0}-|\kappa|}
.
Furthermore, one can obtain

|L^{m}\underline{L}^{n}\partial^{\kappa}\Omega^{p}\phi|\lesssim\delta^{2-\varepsilon_{0}-|\kappa|}\quad\text{for}\ 3m+2n+|\kappa|+p\leq 2N_{0}-3\ \textrm{and $n\geq 1$}.

(B.22)

Indeed, by an induction argument, it suffices to prove (
B.22
) for
3(m+1)+2n+|\kappa|+p\leq 2N_{0}-3
and
n\geq 1
.
It follows from (
B.13
) and the assumption (
B.22
) that

\begin{split}&|L^{m+1}\underline{L}^{n}\bar{\partial}^{\iota}\Omega^{p}\phi|=|L^{m}\underline{L}^{n-1}\bar{\partial}^{\iota}\Omega^{p}(L\underline{L}\phi)|\\
\lesssim&\delta^{2-\varepsilon_{0}-|\iota|}+\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}|\iota_{1}|+p_{1}\leq|\iota|+p+1\\
|\iota_{1}|\leq|\iota|+1,p_{1}\leq p+1\end{array}}\delta^{|\iota_{1}|-|\iota|}|L^{m+1}\underline{L}^{\leq n-1}\bar{\partial}^{\iota_{1}}\Omega^{p_{1}}\phi|.\end{split}

(B.23)

Applying the same argument as for (
B.23
) to treat
|L^{m+1}\underline{L}^{\leq n-1}\bar{\partial}^{\iota_{1}}\Omega^{p_{1}}\phi|
,
one can reduce the number of
\underline{L}
gradually to get

\begin{split}|L^{m+1}\underline{L}^{n}\bar{\partial}^{\iota}\Omega^{p}\phi|&\lesssim\delta^{2-\varepsilon_{0}-|\iota|}+\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}|\iota_{1}|+p_{1}\leq|\iota|+p+n\\
|\iota_{1}|\leq|\iota|+n,p_{1}\leq n+p\end{array}}\delta^{|\iota_{1}|-|\iota|}|L^{m+n}\bar{\partial}^{\iota_{1}}\Omega^{p_{1}}\phi|\\
&\lesssim\delta^{2-\varepsilon_{0}-|\iota|},\end{split}

(B.24)

which yields (
B.22
). Then (
2.53
) is deduced through utilizing (
B.22
) with
\kappa=0
.
Therefore, the proof of Theorem
2.1
is completed.
∎
